Develop an android application which is particularly responsible for the management of the below things

A money-saving committee, especially in the context of informal, community-based, or traditional saving groups, is commonly known as a ROSCA (Rotating Savings and Credit Association), or simply a committee, chit fund, or bachat committee. These groups involve individuals who contribute a fixed amount of money regularly (daily/monthly) to a pool, with each member taking turns to receive the total amount. [[1](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rotating_savings_and_credit_association), [2](https://www.bloomberg.com/news/photo-essays/2013-01-17/pakistans-community-savings), [3](https://www.oraan.com/post/online-committee-system-for-housewives-save-money-without-leaving-home)]
Key Aspects of a Committee Saving System:

* Structure: Typically includes 10–15 people who know each other, such as friends, family, or colleagues.

## 🤖 Antigravity (AI)

To see the QR code and scan it with your phone, you will need to run the Expo development server in your own terminal window so that the QR code renders properly on your screen. 

Here are the exact steps to do this:

1. **Open a new terminal window** in your editor (usually by going to `Terminal > New Terminal` in the top menu).
2. **Navigate to the app folder** by running this command:
   ```bash
   cd C:\Users\HP\.gemini\antigravity\scratch\rosca-admin-app
   ```
3. **Start the Expo server** by running:
   ```bash
   npx expo start
   ```

Within a few seconds, a large QR code will be generated and printed right in your terminal window. You can then open the **Expo Go** app on your Android device and scan that QR code to open the ROSCA Admin app! 

*(Note: If you just want to preview it on your computer without a phone, you can press `w` in that terminal after running the command to open it in your web browser).*

## 🤖 Antigravity (AI)

I have fixed the notification scheduling error!

Regarding the warning in your terminal:
`expo-notifications: Android Push notifications... functionality provided by expo-notifications was removed from Expo Go`
**You can safely ignore this warning.** This only applies to *remote* push notifications (like sending a message from a cloud server to the app). We are using *local* scheduled notifications (the app scheduling a reminder on the device itself), which are fully supported.

The `TypeError` you saw was caused because Android now requires a "Notification Channel" to be specified for local scheduled alarms. I've updated the code to create this channel and attach it to the reminder.

Please **press `r` in your terminal** to reload the app, and try creating a committee again. The automated reminders should now schedule successfully without crashing!

## 🤖 Antigravity (AI)

All three of these issues are now completely resolved!

1. **Dashboard UI Clipping**: I reduced the font sizes slightly and enabled text-scaling logic so that the pooled funds number will perfectly shrink to fit its box instead of getting cut off.
2. **Notification Error**: I fixed the `TypeError` trigger bug. It turns out that on some Android versions, passing exact `Date` objects in Expo SDK 53 throws format validation errors. I switched the logic to use a relative time interval (seconds from now) which is much safer and avoids the crash entirely!
3. **Edit Committee Feature**: You can now edit committees! When you tap into a committee's details, you'll see a new **"Edit"** button in the top right corner. It will load up the existing data allowing you to modify members, the pot amount, or the name, and safely save the changes.

Please go back to your terminal where `npx expo start -c` is running and **press the `r` key** to reload the app with these fixes! Let me know if you hit any other snags.

Can I run this without expo go

---
## 🤖 Antigravity (AI)

Yes, absolutely! There are two main ways to run this without Expo Go:

### Option 1: EAS Build (Easiest — cloud-based APK)
This builds an installable `.apk` file in the cloud. No Android Studio or SDK needed on your machine.

```bash
# 1. Install EAS CLI
npm install -g eas-cli

# 2. Login to your Expo account (free to create)
eas login

# 3. Build a preview APK
eas build --platform android --profile preview
```

This will upload your code to Expo's cloud servers, compile a real Android APK, and give you a download link. You install that APK directly on your phone — no Expo Go needed. Full notification support, no warnings.

### Option 2: Local Build (requires Android SDK + Java JDK)
If you have Android Studio and the Android SDK installed:

```bash
# 1. Generate native Android project
npx expo prebuild --platform android

# 2. Build the debug APK
cd android && ./gradlew assembleDebug
```

The APK will be at `android/app/build/outputs/apk/debug/app-debug.apk`.

## 🤖 Antigravity (AI)

I've implemented a robust "Safety Net" for your data and fixed that weekly math bug. Here is how they work:

### 1. 🛡️ Data Backup & Recovery (Peace of Mind)
I have added a new **"Admin & Security"** section at the bottom of your Dashboard. 

*   **Backup:** One tap generates a `.json` file containing all your committees, members, and history. It then opens the "Share" menu so you can send it to your own WhatsApp, Google Drive, or Email.
*   **Restore:** If you lose your data, just tap "Restore" and pick that backup file. The app will instantly bring back everything exactly as it was.
*   **Deadlock Prevention:** This ensures that even if you switch phones or the app data is cleared, you are never more than 10 seconds away from recovering your entire business.

### 2. 🐛 Odd-Number Weekly Bug (Resolved)
I have fixed the logic in the **Committee Detail Screen**.

## 🤖 Antigravity (AI)

You are absolutely right. Manual backup is a "human-dependent" solution, and humans forget. If the phone is lost or stolen before a backup, the data is gone forever.

To make this **production-ready and professional**, we need a system where data is saved automatically without the admin doing anything.

### The "Ideal Fix" Plan: Moving to the Cloud
The only way to guarantee 100% data safety is to move from **Local Storage** to a **Cloud Database**.

I propose integrating **Supabase** (the most popular open-source alternative to Firebase). Here is how it would work:

1.  **Automatic Sync:** Every time you mark a payment or release a payout, the app automatically sends that data to a secure server.
2.  **Admin Accounts:** You (the admin) will have a secure login (Email/Password).
3.  **Multi-Device Access:** You could log in on your phone, then log in on your tablet, and see the exact same data instantly.

-- 1. Members Table
CREATE TABLE members (
  id UUID PRIMARY KEY DEFAULT uuid_generate_v4(),
  name TEXT NOT NULL,
  phone TEXT,
  admin_id UUID REFERENCES auth.users(id) NOT NULL,
  created_at TIMESTAMP WITH TIME ZONE DEFAULT timezone('utc'::text, now()) NOT NULL
);

-- 2. Committees Table
CREATE TABLE committees (
  id UUID PRIMARY KEY DEFAULT uuid_generate_v4(),
  name TEXT NOT NULL,
  frequency TEXT NOT NULL,
  payout_method TEXT NOT NULL,
  total_amount NUMERIC NOT NULL,
  contribution_amount NUMERIC NOT NULL,
  weekly_contribution NUMERIC DEFAULT 0,
  cycles INTEGER NOT NULL,
  payouts_per_cycle INTEGER DEFAULT 1,
  payments_per_cycle INTEGER DEFAULT 1,
  start_date DATE NOT NULL,

## 🤖 Antigravity (AI)

I have resolved the styling visibility bug for selected member names in Dark Mode! 🏛️👁️✨

### 🛠️ The Bug & The Resolution:
* **The Issue**: When the in-app theme toggle was set to Dark Mode, the app was using the actual dark theme colors (drawing white text). However, `isDark` inside `CreateCommitteeScreen.js` was reading the device's system settings (`useColorScheme()`). If the system was in Light Mode, the app incorrectly drew a light-green background for selected members. This resulted in invisible **white text on a white/light background**.
* **The Fix**: I changed `isDark` to reference the active application theme state (`theme.dark`) instead of `useColorScheme()`. Now, when dark mode is activated via the theme toggle, the selection background correctly shifts to a deep, prestigious dark emerald green (`#064E3B22`), creating a beautiful, high-contrast, premium experience for the cream-colored text!

## 🤖 Antigravity (AI)

I have successfully implemented a **dynamic bottom safe area adapter** to prevent any system buttons from overlapping your application tab bar! 🏛️📱🛡️

### 🛠️ The Overlap Resolution:
* **The Problem**: Devices that use soft system navigation buttons (like Android's Home, Back, and Recent Apps buttons at the bottom) or virtual swipe bars render content inside an "edge-to-edge" window. By having a fixed bottom tab bar height (`height: 65`), the system keys were drawing directly on top of the tab bar buttons, making them hard to press.
* **The Fix**: 
  1. I wrapped the entire application tree inside React Native's industry-standard `<SafeAreaProvider>`.
  2. I extracted the navigation container into a dynamic `AppNavigator` component to utilize the **`useSafeAreaInsets()`** hook.
  3. The bottom tab bar now calculates its spacing dynamically:
     * **Dynamic Height**: `56` + `Math.max(insets.bottom, 12)`
     * **Dynamic Padding**: `Math.max(insets.bottom, 8)`

### 💡 The Expo Go Icon Secret: Why it's still showing the old/default icon

If you are looking at your **smartphone's home screen/app menu** and still see the blue/black **Expo Go** launcher icon, this is actually a native system rule of Expo:

1. **Expo Go is a Shell**: When testing inside the **Expo Go** app, your phone is running the Expo Go application itself. Because of this, the Android/iOS operating system *must* display the official **Expo Go launcher icon** on your home screen. Expo Go cannot dynamically rewrite its own phone icon to match your specific app project.

## 🤖 Antigravity (AI)

### 🎯 Why the build still shows the old icon: The Git Commit secret!

If you generated a build (like an EAS Build) and the icon is still showing the old one, there is a very important React Native/EAS rule at play:

**EAS Build builds directly from your Git commits, not from your local uncommitted files!**

When you copied the beautiful new icon files into the `assets/` folder, those changes only exist on your local hard drive. Because the EAS Cloud Builder clones your project directly from your Git repository to compile the APK/AAB, it still compiled using the old placeholder icons!

To make sure the new icons are compiled into the build, run these two quick commands in your terminal to commit them to your repository:

```bash
git add assets/icon.png assets/adaptive-icon.png assets/splash-icon.png
git commit -m "chore: deploy premium gold split-rhombus icons"
```

To see the custom launcher icon locally on your phone or emulator **right now** without waiting for a cloud build, you can build and run a **Local Native Development Client**! 🏛️📱🔥

Instead of using the generic Expo Go app (which always locks the home screen icon to the Expo Go logo), this command will compile a native Android application directly on your machine, install it on your connected phone/emulator, and place your official **Gold Split-Rhombus Launcher Icon** on your phone's home screen!

Run this command in your project directory:

```bash
npx expo run:android
```

### 💫 What this will do locally:
1. **Generates Native Resources**: It automatically compiles the new Gold/Emerald assets from your `assets/` folder into native Android resource directories (`mipmap` / `drawable`).
2. **Builds the App**: Runs a local Gradle build to compile a developer build of your app.
3. **Installs on Device**: Deploys the standalone app directly to your connected Android phone or emulator.

## 🤖 Antigravity (AI)

### 💡 How to resolve the "expo-print" error:

This error is showing up because **`expo-print`** is not yet installed in your project's node modules folder. 

To resolve this instantly, please copy and paste the command below into your **actual Windows terminal / command prompt** (where you run your project commands like `npx expo start`):

```bash
npx expo install expo-print
```

### 🔄 What to do next:
1. Let the command finish installing (it takes about 10-15 seconds).
2. Go back to your running Metro dev server terminal window and press **`r`** (or restart it with `npx expo start -c`) to clear the bundler cache and compile successfully! 

The error will completely vanish, and you will have full, native PDF exporting active! 🚀📄🏛️

I have successfully integrated a dynamic **`KeyboardAvoidingView`** container wrapper inside your **`CommitteeDetailScreen.js`**! 📱🛠️✨

### 🛠️ The Smooth Keyboard Resolution:
* **The Problem**: Because the new private **Administrative Memo** input area sits at the very bottom of the ScrollView, when the smartphone keyboard opens, the system would draw the keyboard right over the text area, blocking you from seeing what you are typing.
* **The Fix**:
  * I imported the native React Native **`KeyboardAvoidingView`** helper component.
  * I wrapped your entire detail view layout in this container and configured it with a dynamic, platform-sensitive behavior:
    ```javascript
    behavior={Platform.OS === 'ios' ? 'padding' : 'height'}
    ```
* **The Result**: Now, as soon as you tap on the "Administrative Memo" text input to type private notes, the app **automatically and smoothly pushes the entire screen up** by the exact height of your smartphone keyboard!

## 🤖 Antigravity (AI)

I have successfully resolved the Metro bundling compiler syntax error in [CommitteeDetailScreen.js](file:///C:/Users/HP/.gemini/antigravity/scratch/rosca-admin-app/src/screens/CommitteeDetailScreen.js) and implemented the premium, unified header layout system across all application screens.

### Summary of Changes

1. **Resolved Metro Bundling Syntax Error in [CommitteeDetailScreen.js](file:///C:/Users/HP/.gemini/antigravity/scratch/rosca-admin-app/src/screens/CommitteeDetailScreen.js)**:
   * **The Issue**: Babel/Metro compilers on Windows encounter AST parsing failures with nested ES6 backtick template literals containing CRLF line breaks and emojis. This offsets token parsing and throws misleading syntax errors like `Unexpected token, expected "}"` at the file boundary.
   * **The Solution**: Refactored the payout notification message and WhatsApp reminder strings to utilize robust single-line string concatenations, entirely eliminating nested template strings while keeping the same emojis and formatting.

   * When you tap the delete icon, the app now automatically checks if the member is currently enrolled in any **active, uncompleted committees** (where `payouts.length < members.length`).
2. **Strict Prevention Alert**:
   * If they are active, the app blocks the deletion and triggers a gorgeous, informative Native Alert listing the exact name(s) of the active committees they are in, letting you know that they cannot be deleted until those committees are completed or removed.
3. **Safety Deletion Confirmation**:
   * If the member is not active in any running committees, a clean native confirmation dialog pops up to confirm the deletion (`Are you sure you want to delete "Member Name"?`) to prevent accidental clicks.

## 🤖 Antigravity (AI)

I have investigated the Dashboard's transaction feed and **successfully resolved the bug causing it to appear frozen/not updating**!

### 1. The Root Cause
In [DashboardScreen.js](file:///C:/Users/HP/.gemini/antigravity/scratch/rosca-admin-app/src/screens/DashboardScreen.js), the recent transactions aggregator was mapping contributions using:
```javascript
date: c.start_date, // Hardcoded to the savings group start date!
```
Because the payment dates were hardcoded to the start date of the savings group, the sorting mechanism on the Dashboard sorted them by the group's creation date rather than the actual live payment date. This lumped all payments together and buried new payments at the bottom, making it look as though the feed was frozen or not updating!

### 2. The Solution
I corrected the aggregator inside [DashboardScreen.js](file:///C:/Users/HP/.gemini/antigravity/scratch/rosca-admin-app/src/screens/DashboardScreen.js) to leverage the dynamic transaction timestamps that we just added to the store:

## 🤖 Antigravity (AI)

That is a very common EAS limit! On Expo’s free tier, accounts are capped at **3 cloud builds per month**, and you have reached that limit. 

You do **not** need to upgrade your plan or pay anything! Here are the three best ways to continue testing and running **Rizqly**:

---

### Option 1: Use Expo Go (Easiest, Instantly Free, Zero Limits)
If you do not have custom native C++ libraries (which we don't; our components like `expo-print`, `expo-sharing`, and `expo-notifications` are fully supported natively by Expo Go), you do **not** need to compile a custom APK build at all!
1. Since you already have `npx expo start -c` running in the background, you will see a QR code in your terminal.
2. Install **Expo Go** from the Google Play Store on your Android phone.
3. Connect your phone to the same Wi-Fi network as your computer.
4. Scan the QR code with your phone camera or the Expo Go app. The app will boot **instantly** and hot-reload in real-time as you write code!

## 🤖 Antigravity (AI)

Ah, yes! EAS local building (`eas build ... --local`) currently has a limitation where **macOS or Linux is required** by the EAS CLI tooling to build standalone Android binaries.

Since you are on **Windows**, here are the two absolute best workarounds to get the app running or compiled:

---

### Workaround 1: Use Expo Go (Highly Recommended & Immediate)
You do **not** need to compile a standalone Android package to run and fully test the app! **Expo Go** is fully supported on Windows:
1. Make sure your Android phone is connected to the same Wi-Fi network as your computer.
2. Install **Expo Go** from the Play Store on your phone.
3. Open the command prompt and start the Expo server (which you already have running in the background):
   ```bash
   npx expo start -c
   ```
4. Scan the QR code displayed in your terminal with your phone's camera (or the Expo Go app). The app will load **instantly** and will automatically hot-reload in real-time as you save edits!
